# SP48_CW_104: changes and adjudications

A place is counted once against each witness it differs from. Confidence counts are unique changed places, so their sum need not equal C + H. Removing or refining an in-place doubt counts as a change against C; equivalent macro syntax does not. Each reader is identified A, B, or C; witness C is always labelled “witness C”. All observations refer to the supplied scans, without outside research.

## Consequential corrections

- Scan 002, First relation, between Ss sets and Cat: The first relation is a hooked left-pointing arrow, not the primary superset reading of C or the unqualified superset of H.
- Scan 002, Second relation, live heavy arrow entering Cat: The live second relation has a right-hand hook as well as a left arrowhead; H’s straight arrow loses that decoration.
- Scan 002, First short formula, x and y placement: The letters x and y are baseline arguments in Fx and Fy; H makes them subscripts.
- Scan 003, First mu exponent: The first mu superscript is a visible 4; H gives 1 and C leaves 4/1 unresolved.
- Scan 003, Second Fl of last line: Both Fl capitals on the clipped last line are doubled-stem F; both witnesses give the second one as plain F.
- Scan 003, Final index on last line: The final argument has two prime strokes, n-double-prime; H gives one.
- Scan 004, Top Ob label, subscript of C: Top source is Ob C_4, not H’s C_1; the transverse stroke resolves C’s 4/1 doubt.
- Scan 004, Top right arrow target, argument index: The top target argument is looped ell with unresolved n/1 index, not the witnesses’ C.
- Scan 004, First letter of lower replacement term: The live lower replacement has doubled F and ell_1. H omits the live line and the two witnesses use C_1 for its argument.
- Scan 004, F_3 row, sign before third Fl: The F_3 product contains a literal < shaped sign, not H’s multiplication cross; C’s doubt is resolved.
- Scan 004, F_3 row, third argument: Third F_3 argument is a closed looped ell_3, not H’s C_3; C’s l/C doubt is resolved.
- Scan 004, F_n row, relation after initial n: The separator after F_n is literally 2-shaped; the formula is not repaired to equality.
- Scan 004, F_n row, sign between C_1 and the next Fl: After the first C_1 in the F_n row, the visible two-armed wedge is <; both witnesses give multiplication.
- Scan 005, Top chain, leftmost F: The first chain object has doubled F, rather than the witnesses' plain F.
- Scan 005, Top chain, first subscript: The first chain index has n-2 first; n2 and n-1 remain alternatives. No index sequence is supplied.
- Scan 005, Top chain, second and fourth F alphabets: The second and fourth chain F forms are doubled; H's uniform plain alphabet is not retained.
- Scan 005, Open brace and membership below D_n: The open membership brace and its detached equality are retained as unfinished notation.
- Scan 005, Argument glyph throughout upper difference: The four upper arguments are y/f, y first, rather than confidently f.
- Scan 005, First t_n argument and surrounding parentheses: The first inner opening parenthesis is absent, so the formula is not silently balanced.
- Scan 005, Last s_n argument and closing curves: The last inner opening parenthesis is also absent; the two visible closing curves remain.
- Scan 005, Upper left object of small arrow pair: The upper small-map source remains s/f; its letter is not inferred from the map.
- Scan 005, Upper right object of small arrow pair: The small-map target is resolved as t.

### Still unresolved

- First lens, dense loops: Graphical trace; status uncertain; alternatives: Cancelled lettering or nonlinguistic retracing. No dependable letters or defensible word count survive; the source ink itself is traced.
- Second connector, earlier stroke history: \hookleftarrow; alternatives: \deleted{\supset}\hookleftarrow. The live hooked arrow is secure; the older sign is not independently separable.
- Under live Préord: \deleted{\unsure{Ord}}; alternatives: Underlying lettering not independently recoverable. O-like oval and tall d-like stroke are compatible with Ord but overlaid.
- Live third name: Préord; alternatives: PréOrd. The case of the o/O is obscured by overlapping strokes; the acute is clear.
- Lower-right edge: Six nonlinguistic marks; alternatives: Writer’s ink, handling marks or reproduction marks. Marks are visible but cannot be attributed from the supplied image.

### Still unresolved

- Long top horizontal arrow: A securely right-facing arrow with several traced shafts, small left spur and lower branch; earlier writing history unassigned; alternatives: See the explicit local candidates; graphic writing history is unassigned where stated.. The right end contains a dense triangular head. Several longitudinal strokes converge there. The small left spur and the line rising from below-left do not establish an independently cancelled complete left arrow. The reading traces the visible marks rather than declaring an earlier sign deleted.
- Top right arrow target, argument index: \mathrm{Fl}_0(\ell_{\unsure{n|1}}); alternatives: See the explicit local candidates; graphic writing history is unassigned where stated.. The argument is a tall closed ascending loop matching adjacent l, unlike the open C of the factors. Main resolves the letter to ell, pruning C-reader’s C alternatives and rejecting A/B’s fixed C. The low shouldered stroke favours n but also permits 1; it remains doubtful.
- Struck old term above the replacement: \deleted{\unsure{\mathrm{Fl}_0 C_1\times}}; alternatives: See the explicit local candidates; graphic writing history is unassigned where stated.. An F, a looped following letter, a low zero and a cross survive the long crossing stroke. The argument/index is crowded by loops and cannot be separated securely. The uncrossed Ob C_2 belongs to the lower replacement, not to a fully deleted upper product.
- Third row, overwritten underlying first index: \deleted{\unsure{2}} before the live index; alternatives: See the explicit local candidates; graphic writing history is unassigned where stated.. A low curved lobe and foot remain beside the darker upright. A former 2 is plausible, but the shared ink prevents a secure separation of the old numeral from the live one.
- Third row, live first index: \unsure{1|2}; alternatives: See the explicit local candidates; graphic writing history is unassigned where stated.. A diagonal lead rises to a tall dark downstroke, favouring 1; a right lobe and lower horizontal foot also permit 2. The indexed sequence is not used to repair the writing.
- Third row, overwritten material in Ob C_3: \deleted{\unsure{F|\mathrm{Fl}}}\mathrm{Ob}\,C_3; alternatives: See the explicit local candidates; graphic writing history is unassigned where stated.. A tall earlier stem with an upper/middle arm remains inside the O-shaped loop. F is recoverable; an additional old l cannot be isolated from the live b stem. The live Ob and C_3 are secure. Main chooses A’s F-first alternatives over B’s Fl-first order and C-reader’s unqualified old Fl; the old l cannot be isolated securely.
- Isolated X pair, first low index: \unsure{X_0|X_{\varnothing}}; alternatives: See the explicit local candidates; graphic writing history is unassigned where stated.. The small lower loop is crossed by an oblique stroke and by the long connecting trace. A crossed zero and an empty-set-like sign remain possible; the crossing history cannot be separated.
- Isolated X pair, connecting trace: A curve rising between the labels, descending through the lower second X and turning upward to its right; alternatives: See the explicit local candidates; graphic writing history is unassigned where stated.. The trace rises from the first low index toward the second X, drops to a low rounded return, then rises toward the low 1. There is no arrowhead. The reading retains it as a graphic trace with unassigned function.
- Isolated X pair, extra diagonal and upper detached tick: An extra short upper-right diagonal at the first X and a detached short tick above-left of the second X; alternatives: See the explicit local candidates; graphic writing history is unassigned where stated.. The first X has a separate inner diagonal on its upper-right arm. Above-left of the second X is a small dark isolated stroke, separated from the main descending diagonal by white paper. Neither is treated as a lexical accent.
- Isolated right inclusion, overwritten O: \unsure{\deleted{O}\hat{\mathbb F}|\deleted{\mathbb F}\hat O}; alternatives: See the explicit local candidates; graphic writing history is unassigned where stated.. The large O oval and the two F arms/return are both visible, but the static overlap does not establish which was written last. Main adopts C-reader’s explicit two orders; A/B commit to an older O. H acknowledges overlap editorially but omits its body from the formula.
- Lower product, small mark above second cross: A detached curved short tick above the second multiplication cross, traced geometrically; alternatives: See the explicit local candidates; graphic writing history is unassigned where stated.. A small dark upper stroke with a downward curve is separate from the upper-right arm of the cross. Its position can resemble a prime, comma or pen mark; no one lexical value is established.
- Lower product, detached point above-right of third cross: Separate point above-right of the third multiplication cross, traced geometrically; alternatives: See the explicit local candidates; graphic writing history is unassigned where stated.. A dark isolated point lies above-right of the third cross, with a white gap from both cross arms. Its semantic role is unknown.
- Bottom pair row, old loop before final O: \deleted{\unsure{O|0}}; alternatives: See the explicit local candidates; graphic writing history is unassigned where stated.. A larger earlier oval surrounds/extends to the left of the smaller live oval. Its identity as capital O or zero cannot be decided from the shape alone. It is distinct from the initial opening parenthesis.
- Bottom pair row, final O index: \unsure{O_3|O_n}; alternatives: See the explicit local candidates; graphic writing history is unassigned where stated.. The low continuation has a wavy head followed by a pronounced downward curl like the lower part of 3. An n-shaped beginning also survives; overlapping or retraced writing prevents a unique division.
- Bottom pair row, final F index: \unsure{F_n|F_{n+1}|F_3}; alternatives: See the explicit local candidates; graphic writing history is unassigned where stated.. The compressed dark index has an initial oblique stem, an upper triangular counter, a short lower horizontal return and a heavy final downstroke. n and 3 remain possible; a compressed n+1 also cannot be ruled out, but no separate plus and 1 can be securely isolated. Main retains n first (A/C), then n+1 and 3; B’s preferred n+1 cannot be isolated securely and H’s unqualified reading is rejected.
- Final construction, subscript composite letter: \unsure{\deleted{O}\dot{\mathbb F}|\dot B}\times I; alternatives: See the explicit local candidates; graphic writing history is unassigned where stated.. The small sign has a compact oblique raised dot, a doubled/returning stem, an upper arm/counter and a larger oval at right. Main adopts C-reader’s local O/F-composite versus B uncertainty. A/B’s secure old-O-plus-F is not justified; the chronology shares the earlier overlay doubt. The cross and barred I are clear.
- Final construction, surviving oval in last factor: \deleted{O}\hat{\mathbb F} (first candidate of earlier overlay-order doubt); alternatives: See the explicit local candidates; graphic writing history is unassigned where stated.. The large final F still contains an enclosing O-shaped oval crossed by its stem and middle arm. H notes the overlap editorially but does not render the old oval in its formula. Main confirms the doubled return but not a unique overwrite chronology. This occurrence follows the first candidate of the already marked same-page doubt, with an explicit local comment, rather than introducing a new assertion of chronology.
- Detached fleck below/right of that F: Small detached dot traced literally, without a lexical assignment; alternatives: See the explicit local candidates; graphic writing history is unassigned where stated.. A small isolated dark fleck lies at approximately (393,282), below the gap between F and l. Its attachment or semantic purpose cannot be established. It is reproduced as ink, not expanded to an abbreviation or an index.
- F_3 row, third Fl capital: \unsure{\mathbb F|F}\mathrm l; alternatives: See the explicit local candidates; graphic writing history is unassigned where stated.. The third F has a narrow white counter/return along its upper upright, while the lower continuation is not distinctly doubled. All three readers and both witnesses print plain F; main retains the local alphabet doubt with doubled F first.
